We'll compute for each village the maximum effective attack we can achieve, assuming we can select any subset of its troops, and we can also decide to build one extra troop in Castellon (if we choose to build there). We'll also consider we could send troops from multiple villages to gather, but that adds time.

Let's first compute for each village the best possible attack (max) and whether we can get counter bonus.

We'll compute for each village the counts of L, A, C.

We'll consider we can send any number up to those counts.

Goal: maximize effective attack = (sum attack) * multiplier, where multiplier = 1.25 if our most numerous type counters defender's most numerous type, else 1.

We need to consider that the most numerous type depends on composition we send.

Thus we need to find composition that yields highest effective attack given defender type.

We can brute think.

But maybe easier: Since we have many arqueiros, we can often get arqueiro as most numerous. For lanceiro-defender neutrals, arqueiro gives counter bonus. For arqueiro-defender neutrals, we need cavaleiro as most numerous to get bonus; we have limited cavaleiros.

Thus best targets are lanceiro-defender neutrals where we can use arqueiro majority to get bonus.

Lanceiro-defender neutrals: [20], [23], [11], [8] (also maybe [??] check list: [20] 4L, [23]5L, [11]5L, [8]4L). Also [??] maybe [??] none else.

Thus we could aim to attack one of those with arqueiro majority.

We have many arqueiros; we can send from a village with many arqueiros.

Which villages have many arqueiros? [12] 3A, [16] 3A, [14]2A, [13]1A, [19]2A.

Thus we could send from [12] or [16] with 3 arqueiros each.

If we send only arqueiros, our most numerous type = arqueiro (since only arqueiros). That counters lanceiro-defender, giving bonus.

Attack per arqueiro = 2. So if we send N arqueiros, attack = 2N, effective = 2N *1.25 = 2.5N.

We need effective > defense of target.

Defense values for lanceiro-defender neutrals:

[20]: defense 10
[23]: 12.5
[11]:12.5
[8]:10

Thus need 2.5N > defense.

For defense 10: need N > 4 => N >=5 arqueiros.

For defense 12.5: need N >5 => N >=6 arqueiros.

Thus to defeat [20] or [8] we need at least 5 arqueiros. To defeat [23] or [11] need at least 6 arqueiros.

We have villages with at most 3 arqueiros alone. So we need to gather arqueiros from multiple villages to reach 5 or 6.

Thus we need to consolidate.

Alternatively, we could send mixed troops where arqueiros still most numerous but we add some lanceiros or cavaleiros to increase attack while keeping arqueiros as majority.

For example, send 4 arqueiros + 1 lanceiro: total arqueiros 4, lanceiros 1 => arqueiros still most numerous (4>1). Attack = 4*2 +1*1 = 8+1=9. Effective = 9*1.25=11.25 >10 => win vs defense 10 with only 5 total troops (4A+1L). That's better: we need 4 arqueiros + 1 lanceiro.

Thus we need at least 4 arqueiros and at least 1 lanceiro (or any other type that doesn't exceed arqueiro count). So we need to gather at least 4 arqueiros and 1 lanceiro.

We have lanceiros available: [12]2L, [13]2L, [15]2L, others 0.

Arqueiros: as above.

Thus we could gather from multiple villages.

Now we need to consider time to gather.

We could send troops from various villages to a central village (our own) as reinforcement, then send the combined army to target.

The gathering will take time equal to the longest march among the reinforcing troops to the gathering village.

Then after gathering, we send the army to target, taking additional time equal to distance from gathering village to target.

Thus total time = max(gather times) + travel to target.

We could also send directly from multiple villages to target, but they'd fight separately; not good.

Thus we need to pick a gathering village that is relatively central.

We could choose Castellon ([15]) as gathering point because we can also build a troop there.
